● Full suite passes (1519/1519; the "ASSERTION FAILED" lines are expected

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Step 1 is done with one fix. I worked out the orchestration myself, and it
  runs in the test suite with only one change needed outside the tests. The full
  suite passes (1519/1519) and eslint is clean. I haven't tried it on the real
  tldraw canvas yet.

  Step 1: the composed widget

  This is what the AI should produce: a mixin onto the image generator, as the
  issue describes.

  fluid.def("fluid.demos.ai.closestNoteImageGenerator", {
      $layers: ["fluid.demos.imageGenerator", "fluid.topLevelWidget"],
      noteFinder: {
          $component: {
              $layers: "fluid.demos.closestNoteFinder",
              view: "{closestNoteImageGenerator}.view"
          }
      },
      promptFromNote: {
          $effect: {
              func: (state, noteText) => {
                  state.prompt = noteText;
              },
              args: ["{self}.state", "{noteFinder}.noteText"]
          }
      }
  });

  A new test, "Closest note composed with image generator", in
  tests/framework-tests/from-demos/js/TldrawAITests.js checks that:
  - The note finder shares the image generator's view, so its own template is
    never drawn.
  - The note finder gets the composed widget's shape bounds and finds the
    nearest note.
  - The prompt follows that note's text, including when the note is edited, and
    "Find Image" searches Pixabay for it.
  - The widget can be saved as a PTF document and rebuilt from it with nothing
    broken.
  - The "umbrella holding both widgets" form described in the AI's current hints
    also works.

  The fix: the framework handled all of this correctly. The problem was in the
  demo's fluid.tldraw.ai.diagnose, which reported the hidden note finder's
  distanceLine view as broken ("Container not specified"). Its results go back
  to the AI as problems to repair, so every correct answer would have set off
  pointless repair attempts. Views under a component whose view comes from
  another component are now treated as hidden, not broken
  (demo/tldraw/js/ai-protocol.js). Errors in their templates are still reported,
  and the existing check for a view with no container still passes.

  I haven't rebuilt the copies under docs/ and dist/.

  Step 2: what's missing

  1. The likeliest mistake fails silently. Following the hint's input: 
     "{first}.output" example, a model will probably write state: {prompt: 
     "{noteFinder}.noteText"}. I tried it: the reference ends up next to
     $reactiveRoot in the merged state and is ignored. The prompt stays empty
     and nothing is reported, so the repair loop gets no feedback. A single
     layer that puts extra keys beside $reactiveRoot fails loudly, but the
     merged result doesn't. The framework should either reject this or give it a
     meaning, and that choice is yours. This is the main gap.
  2. The hints don't describe this pattern. They cover the umbrella-of-two form
     and cross-injecting a reference into a plain member. They don't mention the
     issue's preferred mixin onto the main widget, or using $effect to push a
     value into another widget's reactive state. The IFM's docs do explain
     $effect.
  3. "These two selected widgets" is already covered. The selection description
     names each selected widget's shape id and layer, and the workspace document
     includes both layer definitions.
  4. Undo interacts with the effect. Each note change writes state.prompt, which
     the canvas records as an undoable step. Undoing it reverts the prompt even
     though the note hasn't changed, and it stays out of step until the note
     changes again. It's minor, but worth deciding before the demo.
  5. Real-canvas check still needed: real bounds, live notes, the widget
     avatars, persistence and pasting.

  I can turn the solution into a ready PTF response to paste onto the canvas for
  that check.

✻ Cooked for 5m 43s · done Wednesday 15:41

※ recap: Goal is issue #25: composing the closest-note finder with the image generator. Step 1 works and its tests pass, but I haven't checked it on the real canvas. Next, decide how a reference merged into reactive state (`state.prompt`) should behave, since it's currently silently ignored. (disable recaps in /config)

❯ "A single layer that puts extra keys beside $reactiveRoot fails loudly, but   
  the merged result doesn't. The framework should either reject this or give it 
  a meaning," - to be clear, do you mean "extra keys beside $reactiveRoot"      
  something that violates JSON by duplicating keys, or something else - could   
  you write out what this faulty definition would look like? When you say, "the 
  merged result doesn't" - would the fault be removable by allowing the         
  $reactiveRoot status at a path to be merged away by a further definition,     
  rather than it being a marker that sticks irremovably in the shadowMap        
  forever?                                                                      

● No, nothing here duplicates JSON keys. I meant two different keys,
  $reactiveRoot and prompt, sitting side by side in the same state object.

  Written in one layer, the faulty definition looks like this:

  fluid.def("my.widget", {
      state: {
          $reactiveRoot: {prompt: "", isGenerating: false},
          prompt: "{noteFinder}.noteText"
      }
  });

  fluid.extractPolicy (src/framework/core/js/Fluid.js:2205) catches this with
  fluid.fail("Unexpected extra keys together with $reactiveRoot: ..."). That's
  "fails loudly", though I got it from reading the code rather than running it.

  Split across two layers, which is what a model would naturally write, the same
  thing gets through. The image generator defines state: {$reactiveRoot: 
  {prompt: "", ...}}, and the mixin adds:

  fluid.def("fluid.tests.naive", {
      $layers: ["fluid.demos.imageGenerator", "fluid.topLevelWidget"],
      state: {
          prompt: "{noteFinder}.noteText"
      }
  });

  Each layer passes extractPolicy on its own. Merging works key by key, so the
  result is the shape I printed in the probe:

  {"$reactiveRoot": {"prompt": "", "isGenerating": false, ...}, "prompt":
  "{noteFinder}.noteText"}

  That's the shape the single-layer check rejects, but nothing checks it after
  merging. The reactive record is built only from the $reactiveRoot contents, so
  the sibling prompt is silently dropped. That's what "the merged result
  doesn't" meant.

  Would letting a later layer merge the $reactiveRoot status away fix it? It
  would remove the silent drop, but I don't think it gives this case the meaning
  we want:

  - The image generator needs state to stay writable. generate, showAnother and
    refresh all write into state, and the input's two-way binding writes
    state.prompt. If the mixin's state: {...} removed the root status at state,
    those writes would have nowhere to go. You'd also need a rule for whether
    the base's $reactiveRoot contents get merged in as plain values.
  - The marker covers a whole path. It sits at state, so removing it can only
    take away state as a whole. Here we want state to stay a reactive root and
    only state.prompt to follow the note.
  - The marker seems meant to stick. The comment on extractPolicy says a marker
    "merged over by a live or other layer will still need to be honoured". I
    read that as live parts and restored persistence, which write plain state
    values, relying on it. So if removal were allowed, it would have to be
    limited to definition layers, not live ones. I'm going by the comment there
    and haven't traced how live parts are actually merged.

  What the mixin really means is a reactive value seeded from a reference: it
  follows {noteFinder}.noteText until something writes to it, and follows again
  when the note changes. That's what the $effect in my solution does, but as
  hand-written imperative wiring.

  So I see two options:
  - Give it that meaning: a reference merged over a member of a reactive root
    becomes a writable value that tracks its source. The naive form would then
    just work.
  - Reject it: run the extra-keys check on the merged result as well, giving an
    error that points to $effect. diagnose would surface that to the AI for
    repair.

  The first gives the most "slop-free" composition, but it's a semantic decision
  for the framework, so it's your call.
✻ Churned for 23s · done Wednesday 16:12

❯ Yup, fix the framework's semantics so a path may contain a reference as well  
  as being or containing reactive values. This is a kind of semantic hole in    
  the framework in any case - the $reactiveRoot syntax was somewhat hurriedly   
  designed and it really belongs, along with a lot of other "out of band"       
  information such as types, in some other class of expression. The fact that   
  we reject an all-at-once definition and don't reject the merged variant of it 
  adds further weight to the fact that both variants should be blessed and      
  supported.                                                                    

● Starting by tracing how $reactiveRoot flows through merging, expansion and
  writes.

  Ran 4 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Key question is how the live layer interacts with merged state once a reactive
  root is written. Reading that path.

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● The live layer overwrites the merged state with its signal on first write, so
  links need to come from the layers' policy pass (e.g. the reactiveRoot marker)
  to persist. I'll check how effects are scheduled and disposed next.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● I've worked out the merge/expansion plan: siblings of $reactiveRoot will
  overlay or link into the root instead of being rejected, with links becoming

● Both pass. Full suite and lint again.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Both forms now work, so the composition needs no $effect. The mixin simply
  says:

  state: {
      prompt: "{noteFinder}.noteText"
  }

  The full suite passes (1536/1536) and eslint is clean. The browser-only tests
  and the real canvas are still unchecked.

  How it behaves now

  Writing extra members next to $reactiveRoot, whether in a later layer or the
  same one, is no longer ignored or rejected:

  - A plain value (e.g. nested: {size: 5}) replaces that member's starting
    value, merged in deeply.
  - A reference links the member to its source. The member takes the source's
    value whenever the source changes, and can still be written as before.
    Whichever happened last, a source change or a write, decides the value. So
    you can type into the prompt, and the next change to the closest note takes
    over again.
  - Writes made by a link are left out of the undo history. A user's own writes
    are still recorded, so undo goes back to the linked value.
  - Links keep working after the root has been written. Previously, once state
    was written the live layer replaced the merged state entirely; links now
    survive that.
  - A stronger layer can switch a link off by giving the member a value inside
    its own $reactiveRoot.
  - Data inside $reactiveRoot is never a link. A stored string like
    "{not}.aReference" stays plain data.

  How it's implemented

  - Fluid.js: the single-layer "extra keys" check is gone. A new
    fluid.extractReactiveLinks runs alongside extractPolicy and records each
    root's links in the layerMap. It reads every layer except the live one,
    which is why links survive the live layer taking over.
  - FluidIL.js: transferShadowMap sets up each link as an effect at the member's
    path. The reference string itself is the effect's identity, so the effect
    is kept, not rebuilt, when the component recomputes. expandReactiveRecord
    now receives the whole merged element so it can apply plain-value overrides.
  - FluidView.js: writeableAttrSite had been treating any site with an effect
    record as read-only. Linked members are now excluded from that, so the input
    still writes back.

  Things to review

  - The first version broke the todo-list-assign demo. fluid.UISelect has
    optionValues: {$reactiveRoot: []}, which a stronger layer replaces with a
    $compute. I had wrongly read the $compute's args string as a link.
    - Now, a directive like $compute at the root path replaces the whole root,
      as it always did.
    - A directive on a single member, e.g. state: {colour: "$compute:..."}, now
      fails loudly with a message naming the member. It used to be silently
      dropped.
    - Linking members to $compute results the same way would be a natural next
      step; I left it out.
  - Restoring a saved widget keeps its typed value. The link doesn't overwrite
    it until the note next changes. The tldraw test now checks for this.
  - AI hint updated: I added one sentence to the strategy hints in
    ai-protocol.js saying a reference can now override a single member of a
    widget's reactive state.
  - Not done: the docs in your ponder repo still describe $reactiveRoot the old
    way, and I haven't rebuilt the copies under docs/ and dist/.

  New tests are in tests/framework-tests/core/js/FluidILTests.js (links, plain
  values, single layer, overridden links, directive cases). The issue #25 test
  in TldrawAITests.js now uses the plain-reference form for both the mixin and
  the umbrella, including typing into the linked input.

      372 +* Often one widget of a composition is **dominant** - it shows
          + the result which the user wants to see and act on - \        
      373 +whilst the other only **supplies** a value to it, e.g. a widge
          +t finding some text which the dominant widget should \        
      374 +act on. The most economical geometry is then a fresh layer whi
          +ch extends the dominant widget's layer directly and \         
      375 +mixes in the supplier as a hidden subcomponent, with three ing
          +redients and nothing else:                                    
      376 +    1. \`$layers\` naming the dominant widget's existing layer
          + followed by \`fluid.topLevelWidget\`, so that the fresh \    
      377 +widget IS the dominant widget, with all of its template, state
          + and methods.

● I've added a hint to the AI's strategy hints in demo/tldraw/js/ai-protocol.js
  that lays out this kind of solution, along with a test that runs its example.
  Adding that test exposed an old framework bug, which I've fixed. The full
  suite passes (1544/1544) and eslint is clean.

  The new hint

  It sits after the existing umbrella hint and describes the case where one
  widget is dominant (it shows the result the user acts on) and the other only
  supplies a value to it. The recommended shape is a fresh layer with exactly
  three parts:

  1. Extend the dominant widget: $layers names its existing layer followed by
     fluid.topLevelWidget, so the new widget is the dominant one, with its
     template, state and methods.
  2. Hide the supplier: the supplier becomes a subcomponent whose view points at
     the fresh widget's own view. The hint explains that a layer's context name
     is the last segment of its name. It also says that the supplier and its
     subcomponents then render nothing but keep computing, including anything
     that depends on where the widget is placed.
  3. Wire them: a reference written at the dominant widget's input path, which
     may be a member of its reactive state.

  It then gives a worked example with made-up names, so it doesn't hand the IFM
  the issue #25 answer:

  fluid.def("my.ai.showsFoundResult", {
      $layers: ["my.resultShower", "fluid.topLevelWidget"],
      finder: {
          $component: {
              $layers: "my.textFinder",
              view: "{showsFoundResult}.view"
          }
      },
      state: {
          query: "{finder}.foundText"
      }
  });

  It closes with what not to do:
  - Don't copy templates, styles or functions from the existing layers.
  - Don't write an $effect or $method to pass values between them.
  - Send the layer as a text/javascript part.
  - Create one fresh widget, and leave the original widgets in place unless the
    user asks to replace them.

  Testing the example

  A new test, "Worked example of composition in the strategy hints is valid",
  pulls the fluid.def out of the hint text and evaluates it. It defines stand-in
  my.resultShower and my.textFinder layers; the finder's output depends on its
  placement and it has a subcomponent with its own view. The test checks that
  nothing is diagnosed, that the input follows the finder's output, and that
  neither the finder nor its subcomponent renders anything.

  Bug fixed along the way

  This test broke the issue #25 test that runs after it. It creates and destroys
  a tldrawHost, and afterwards any {tldrawHost} reference still resolved to the
  destroyed instance. The code had a TODO: Remember to delete these again when 
  clearing at the point where resolveRoot components register in the root scope,
  and those entries were never removed. When a component is destroyed, its own
  scope is emptied first, so clearScope no longer knew which names to remove.

  In FluidIL.js, each component now records the scope names it has registered:
  - Names it no longer holds are withdrawn from its parent's scope and, for
    resolveRoot components, from the root scope.
  - clearScope uses that record on destroy.

  The new IL test "Destroyed resolveRoot component withdrawn from root scope"
  covers this. One limit remains: if two resolveRoot instances are alive at once
  and the first is destroyed, the second doesn't take over the name. That's the
  existing "chain a linked list" TODO, which I didn't address.

  I also declared my as a global for eslint in TldrawAITests.js.

✻ Cooked for 5m 24s · done Wednesday 16:31

❯ Echo here what the AI's layer should look like                                

● For the issue #25 widgets, this is the layer, as verified in TldrawAITests.js:

  fluid.def("fluid.demos.ai.closestNoteImageGenerator", {
      $layers: ["fluid.demos.imageGenerator", "fluid.topLevelWidget"],
      noteFinder: {
          $component: {
              $layers: "fluid.demos.closestNoteFinder",
              view: "{closestNoteImageGenerator}.view"
          }
      },
      state: {
          prompt: "{noteFinder}.noteText"
      }
  });

  - $layers makes the new widget the image generator itself, so it keeps the
    image generator's template, state, search, "Another" button and error
    handling.
  - noteFinder adds the closest note finder as a subcomponent sharing the
    generator's view. It renders nothing, including its distance line, but keeps
    finding the nearest note to the new widget's own shape.
  - state.prompt links the generator's prompt to the closest note's text. "Find
    Image" then searches for that text, and the user can still type over it
    until the note changes.

  In the IFM's reply, this would go in a text/javascript layer part named
  fluid.demos.ai.closestNoteImageGenerator. It would be followed by a component
  part with a fresh name such as new-1, holding a potentia part for / of
  {"$layers": ["fluid.demos.ai.closestNoteImageGenerator"]}. The two original
  widgets stay on the canvas.

  The context name in view could also be {imageGenerator}, since the new widget
  carries that layer too. I'd keep the new layer's own name for clarity, and
  it's what the hint teaches.

    image button pressed, with a fake Pixabay recording what it searches for.

    leak into another.

    the model. It scored 9/9 and worked, which shows the checks themselves are

  ┌───────────────────────────────────────┬───────┬───────────┬─────────────┐
  │                Variant                │ Works │  Fit vs   │  Exchanges  │
  │                                       │       │   ideal   │             │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────────┼─────────────┤
  │ With the new composition hint (3 + 3  │ 6/6   │ 9/9 ×5,   │ 1 each,     │
  │ from the earlier batch)               │       │ 8/9 ×1    │ 6–18s       │
  ├───────────────────────────────────────┼───────┼───────────┼─────────────┤
  │ Without the hint                      │ 0/3   │ 7/9, 4/9, │ 1–2, 18–30s │
  │                                       │       │  5/9      │             │
  └───────────────────────────────────────┴───────┴───────────┴─────────────┘

  With the hint, the responses match the ideal layer apart from the layer and
  member names. The one 8/9 (latest batch, sample 2) is still correct. It routed
  the note text through a small extra $compute that blanks the prompt when no
  note is near. It's not quite minimal, but it's arguably a refinement. No
  response copied code, redefined an existing layer or touched the original
  widgets.

  Without the hint, the model fell back to heavier designs, and every one failed
  for a different reason. Crucially, none of these failures were reported as
  problems, so the repair loop never got a chance to fix them:

  1. Sample 1 (fit 7/9): its template override was ignored. It extended both
     layers, supplied its own view template, and kept the prompt in step with an
     $effect. Its first reply also sent an SFC as text/javascript, which the
     repair loop fixed. But the note finder's SFC template rendered instead of
     the new one, so there was no input or button to press. This is a framework 
     gap: a derived layer can't override an SFC widget's template.
  2. Samples 2 and 3 (fit 4/9 and 5/9): the note finder never knew where it was.
     Both used the "umbrella with its own template" form that the existing hint
     allows, and showed both widgets. The nested note finder said "Not placed
     on a canvas". A top-level widget nested inside another gets no host bounds,
     because only the outer widget's view is hosted. This is a second
     framework/demo gap. Sample 3 even wired state: {prompt: 
     "{finder}.closest.text"} correctly, and would have worked if the finder
     knew its position.
  3. Both also deleted the original widgets. Sample 2 also replaced the
     generator's generate and cannotGenerate with copies of its own.

  What this means for step 2

  - The prompt now works with this model. The direction, the selection
    description and the hint together get K2 to the ideal answer reliably and
    quickly.
  - The hint is doing the work. Without it the model doesn't find the "dominant
    widget plus hidden supplier" pattern. The no-hint reasoning also showed it
    expects a reference to write back into its source, until the hint says
    otherwise.
  - Gaps that only showed up through the no-hint failures:
    - Nested top-level widgets should inherit their host bounds from the widget
      that encloses them. That would make the umbrella form work too.
    - A derived layer's view template should override an SFC's template, or at
      least fail loudly.
    - diagnose can't catch a widget that renders but doesn't do the job. For
      coaching the IFM, these harness trials are the real signal.

  The harness

  It's in a-scratch/ifm-harness/. Run it from the repo root with node 
  a-scratch/ifm-harness/run-harness.js [samples].

  - Options: HARNESS_VARIANT=nohint removes the composition hint.
    HARNESS_DRYRUN=1 runs the ideal response through instead of calling the
    model, to check the harness itself.
  - Output: each sample folder in runs/ holds the exact request-payload.json
    sent, every system prompt, response and reasoning, the transcript, and an
    assessment.json with the fit scores, the trials and the rendered markup.
  - Rate limit: the proxy rate-limits beyond about 3 samples at once.
  - Fidelity fixes this round: the stand-in host layers now use the demo's real
    names, so the payload is identical to the demo's. The check for redefined
    layers now compares against what actually existed beforehand. Replacing a
    widget that's still loading no longer crashes.

  ifm-password.txt is now in .gitignore. The runs/ folders hold full system
  prompts of about 100KB each; you may want to gitignore them too.

● Starting with the template override gap: tracing how an SFC's template
  attaches to its view.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● I found that SFC layers patch their view to derive from
  fluid.templateViewComponent, which makes templateLayer's templateTree always
  take priority over one computed from a derived layer's template. I'll review
  every usage of templateLayer before deciding on a fix.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)
● I'll fix this by having the SFC loader write its template into view.template
  as a small reference record instead of a separate templateLayer, letting
  ordinary layer merging decide precedence. Now I'll check the tests that pin
  the current patching behavior.

  Ran 2 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Both framework gaps are fixed and the full suite passes (1555/1555). The IFM
  harness is now rerunning on the no-hint and standard prompts to see whether
  more of the model's own solutions work; I'll report when it finishes.

  1. A layer derived from an SFC widget can now replace its template. Previously
  the SFC loader stored the template reference in a separate templateLayer
  member, and a layer derived from it (fluid.sfcTemplateViewComponent) always
  rendered it. The loader now writes the reference into the view's template
  member itself, as {sfcLayer: "<layer>"}.
  - fluid.templateViewComponent turns either markup or that reference into a
    tree via a new fluid.templateToTree.
  - Normal merging now decides which template wins. A derived layer's template
    string overrides an inherited SFC template, and a derived SFC's own template
    overrides its parent's.
  - fluid.sfcTemplateViewComponent stays, but only as a tag layer.
  - The editor's template-layer lookup in editorRoot.js reads the new form, and
    the tests that pin the patched SFC shape (FluidPTFTests.js,
    FluidViewSFCTests.js) are updated.

  2. A top-level widget nested inside another now knows where it is.
  - In FluidView.js, fluid.topLevelWidget now takes its view's reported
    hostBounds when there are any, and otherwise those of the nearest enclosing
    top-level widget. A new enclosingHostBounds member follows that ancestor's
    bounds, through any intermediate components.
  - In tldraw-driver.js, the tldraw host now reports null bounds for a view it
    doesn't host. Before, it reported unavailable, which breaks the "null if its
    host does not report any" contract on fluid.topLevelWidgetView.

  New tests:
  - FluidViewTests.js: a widget nested two levels down, via an intermediate
    component, takes its umbrella's bounds and loses them when the umbrella
    stops being hosted.
  - TldrawAITests.js "Further compositions chosen by the AI":
    - The no-hint IFM's form works: an umbrella with its own template showing
      both widgets, with state: {prompt: "{finder}.closest.text"}. The finder
      shows the closest note and the prompt follows it.
    - A layer derived from fluid.demos.closestNoteFinder with its own template
      renders that template instead of the SFC's.

  The two eslint errors in editorRoot.js (lines 75 and 142) were there before;
  my edit there is only the template lookup. I haven't rebuilt the copies under
  docs/ and dist/.

  - Sample 1: a wrapper widget with its own template showing both widgets, wired
    with a plain reference ({finder}.noteText) and no hand-written code. This
    needed the nested-widget position fix.
  - Sample 2: the same wrapper shape, but wired with an $effect. It also needed
    the nested-widget position fix.
  - Sample 3: extends both existing widgets and supplies its own template plus a
    $method. This needed the template-override fix.

  These are heavier than the ideal and mostly deleted or replaced the original
  widgets, but they all do what you asked.

  A new demo bug turned up, now fixed. One hinted reply tried to switch off the
  note finder's distance line by writing distanceLine: false. The framework
  rightly refused to merge that, but the AI layer's problem checker then crashed
  on the broken widget instead of reporting it. In the real demo that would
  have aborted the conversation instead of letting the repair loop fix it. The
  checker now reports the merge error itself, e.g. "Cannot merge value false at
  path distanceLine…", which goes back to the model as something it can correct.
  It's covered in the "Problems diagnosed in widgets" test.

  Other observations from this run:
  - Originals deleted: both hinted replies deleted the two original widgets this
    time, despite the hint asking them to be left unless the user wants them
    replaced. In the earlier 6 hinted runs none did, so the model doesn't follow
    this reliably.
  - Repair loop: one hinted reply first sent badly nested PTF, and the repair
    loop fixed it within two rounds.

  Harness: it now retries failed DNS lookups and 429 rate limits with backoff,
  which saved several samples in this run.

● The 429 came from your IFM proxy,
  https://live.ponder.org.uk/ifm/v1/chat/completions, the endpoint
  fluid.tldraw.ai.callEndpoint posts to. Pixabay was never involved, because the
  harness fakes those calls.

  It happened only once, in sample 2 of the first real run
  (runs/2026-10-21T15-42-24-191Z), when I fired 4 samples at the same moment.
  The body was:

  {"error":{"message":"Request rate limit exceeded","type":"rate_limit_error","p
  aram":null,"code":"rate_limit_exceeded"}}

  A second file matching "429" was a false hit: a source offset in a log line,
  not a response.

  After that I ran at most 3 samples at once, and no further 429s appeared. All
  6 retries in the later runs were DNS lookup failures (EAI_AGAIN) on
  live.ponder.org.uk. So my earlier line that the retries were "saving samples
  from 429s" overstated it: they only saved samples from DNS failures.

  From the response alone I can't tell whether the limit is set in your proxy or
  passed through from the IFM service behind it. The proxy's configuration
  would show that. Four simultaneous requests of about 23k input tokens each
  were enough to trigger it once.

● The script is ready. The 24 samples are running in the background; I'll report
  when they finish.

● The prompt is reordered and the experiment is running in the background. It's
  24 samples: the new and old orders, each with and without the hint, 6 samples
  per combination. I'll report the comparison when it finishes.

  The prompt change (demo/tldraw/js/ai-protocol.js):
  - New order: introduction, documentation, response rules, strategy hints, then
    the workspace, then the user's selection.
  - Caching: everything before # The workspace is now the same for every
    request. That's the prefix fluid.tldraw.ai.cachedTokens already counts as
    cached.
  - Wording: two lines that referred to position now say "at the end of this
    prompt" instead of "above", and the introduction's description of the layout
    matches the new order.
  - Tests: I updated the section-order check in TldrawAITests.js to the new
    order. The AI tests pass 234/234 and lint is clean. I haven't re-run the
    full npm test suite since this change.

  Harness changes:
  - HARNESS_ORDER=old restores the previous order, as a control. With only the
    old runs to compare against, the results would be confounded, because those
    runs were made against several different states of the framework.
  - Each sample now records its hint setting and prompt order in variant.txt.
  - Dry runs score 9/9 in both orders, so the harness itself works.

  Six samples per combination is small. Unless one condition fails outright, the
  result will show only a large effect.

        1 +The user wants to combine the two selected widgets:           
        2 +1. `fluid.demos.closestNoteFinder` - finds the closest sticky 
          +note to the widget, shows its text, and the distance          
        3 +2. `fluid.demos.imageGenerator` - generates an image from a pr
          +ompt                                                          
        4 +                                                              
        5 +The goal: move the combined widget close to a sticky note, and
          + press a button to generate an image. Presumably, the combined
          + widget should use the closest note's text as the prompt for t
          +he image generator.                                           
        6 +                                                              
        7 +So this is a composition: the imageGenerator is dominant (show
          +s the image), and the closestNoteFinder supplies the note text
          + as the prompt. Or alternatively, the closestNoteFinder is the
          + display showing which note was used... Let me think about wha
          +t the user wants.                                             
        8 +                                                              
        9 +"Combine the functionality of the two selected widgets so I ca
          +n move it close to a sticky note and press a button to generat
          +e an image"                                                   
       10 +                                                              
       11 +So: the widget finds the closest sticky note (its text), uses 
          +that text as the prompt, and when you press a button, generate
          +s an image. So the dominant widget is the image generator — it
          + shows the image. The closestNoteFinder supplies `noteText` as
          + the prompt.                                                  
       12 +                                                              
       13 +Per the strategy hints, the most economical geometry is:      
       14 +1. A fresh layer extending the dominant widget's layer (fluid.
          +demos.imageGenerator) followed by fluid.topLevelWidget        
       15 +2. A subcomponent from the supplier's layer (fluid.demos.close
          +stNoteFinder) whose view is a reference to the fresh widget's 
          +own view so it doesn't render                                 
       16 +3. Wiring from supplier's output to dominant widget's input — 
          +the state member `prompt` gets `"{finder}.noteText"`.         
       17 +                                                              
       18 +Wait, but there's a subtlety: the imageGenerator's prompt is a
          + `$reactiveRoot` state under `state.prompt`, bound two-way to 
          +the input `value="@{state.prompt}"`. If we wire `state: {promp
          +t: "{finder}.noteText"}`, then the input will display the note
          + text, and it's still writable (the member takes the reference
          +d value whenever it changes, and may still be written as befor
          +e).                                                           
       19 +                                                              
       20 +Hmm, but should the wiring be to `state.prompt` directly? The 
          +state is `$reactiveRoot`:                                     
       21 +                                                              
       22 +```js                                                         
       23 +state: {                                                      
       24 +    $reactiveRoot: {                                          
       25 +        prompt: "",                                           
       26 +        isGenerating: false,                                  
       27 +        ...                                                   
       28 +    }                                                         
       29 +}                                                             
       30 +```                                                           
       31 +                                                              
       32 +If I override `state: {prompt: "{finder}.noteText"}` in the de
          +rived layer, the merge rules say: "A key holding a signal reco
          +rd is replaced as a unit by a stronger layer's value at that k
          +ey" — but here `state` is a `$reactiveRoot` record, a signal r
          +ecord. Replacing it would replace the whole reactive root. But
          + `prompt: "{finder}.noteText"` — under variables/live preceden
          +ce, a plain value at a member of the reactive root... The reac
          +tive root's contents are merged? The `$reactiveRoot` record is
          + one key, so overriding `state` wholesale would lose the other
          + keys.                                                        
       33 +                                                              
       34 +Actually, per merge rules: "Plain objects contributed by more 
          +than one layer merge recursively, key by key. Any other value 
          +(primitive, array, function, signal record) from a stronger la
          +yer replaces that from a weaker one wholesale." `state` holds 
          +a signal record `{$reactiveRoot: {...}}` in the weaker layer. 
          +If my stronger layer contributes `prompt: "..."` at `$layers` 
          +scope (def precedence, weaker than user but the derived layer 
          +beats the base), then... my layer's `state` would be `{prompt:
          + "{finder}.noteText"}` — a plain object, not a signal record. 
          +How do plain objects merge with signal records? Hmm.

● Moving the invariant material to the front made no noticeable difference to
  the model's results; whether the hint is included still decides how good they
  are. I ran 6 samples for each combination of old order or new, hint or no hint
  (24 in all) against the current framework.

  ┌───────────────┬───────┬───────────────────────────┬────────────────────┐
  │    Prompt     │ Works │         Mean fit          │        Fits        │
  ├───────────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Hint, new     │ 5/6   │ 7.0/9 (8.6 for the five   │ 8, 9, 9, 8, 8, 0   │
  │ order         │       │ that worked)              │                    │
  ├───────────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ Hint, old     │ 5/6   │ 8.3/9                     │ 8, 9, 8, 8, 9, 8   │
  │ order         │       │                           │                    │
  ├───────────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ No hint, new  │ 6/6   │ 4.3/9                     │ 3, 5, 4, 4, 6, 4   │
  │ order         │       │                           │                    │
  ├───────────────┼───────┼───────────────────────────┼────────────────────┤
  │ No hint, old  │ 1/6   │ 2.3/9 over the 4 that     │ 4, 0, 0, 5, plus 2 │
  │ order         │       │ finished                  │  crashes           │
  └───────────────┴───────┴───────────────────────────┴────────────────────┘

  - Order: with the hint, both orders work 5 times out of 6 and score 8–9/9 when
    they work. The new order's one failure was a cut-off reply (explained
    below), not a worse design.
  - Hint: it makes the big difference. With it, answers are nearly ideal;
    without it, they're 3–6/9 umbrellas that copy templates and add their own
    glue code.
  - No hint, old order: the 1/6 there is not caused by the order. Both crashes
    and both empty results came from bugs or limits that hit both orders, by
    chance in that group. Six samples per group can't separate it from the new
    order's 6/6.
  - Caching: I couldn't confirm it. The proxy reported no cached tokens on
    either of two back-to-back real requests. The reorder costs nothing either
    way.

  The failures exposed three problems, which I fixed:

  1. Out-of-memory crash on a self-reference. Two answers gave the finder
     subcomponent hostBounds: "{self}.hostBounds", meaning the umbrella. Inside
     a $component, {self} is the subcomponent itself, so the value referred to
     itself and node ran out of memory. A reference to its own path (or one
     containing it) now resolves to an error that says so and explains what
     {self} means there.
     - The underlying bug was again the traversal reusing its path array after
       the reference captured it; fluid.fetchContextReference now copies it.
     - Without that line the same answer works, at 5/9.
  2. Problems inside new widgets were never reported back. A new widget that was
     built successfully was never checked, so the self-reference never reached
     the repair loop. tldraw-ai.js and the harness now check new widgets after
     they settle, and diagnose reports members that failed. Each cause is
     reported only once, so one broken value doesn't produce a line for
     everything that depends on it.
  3. Cut-off replies were shown to the user as answers. Three replies stopped at
     exactly 8192 output tokens, partway through the model's reasoning.
     - Requests now set max_tokens: 32768. A probe confirmed the proxy accepts
       this, and two later answers ran to 9,393 and 15,545 tokens without being
       cut off.
     - A reply that does stop at the limit is no longer shown as an answer: the
       model is told and asked to resend.
     - The proxy's cached_tokens count is now read when present, and the panel
       shows it in place of its estimate.

  The full suite passes (1569/1569), with new tests for each fix and an updated
  ordering test. ESLint is clean on the JavaScript I changed, but it can't parse
  tldraw-ai.js or the .vue file, so those two weren't linted. npm run lint
  fails only on generated .d.mts files and CSS that this work didn't touch.

  I haven't re-run the 24 samples since these fixes, so they apply equally to
  both orders but the table above predates them. The runs are in
  a-scratch/ifm-harness/runs/2026-10-21T22-15… to 22-33…. The harness has two
  new settings: HARNESS_ORDER=old restores the old prompt order, and
  HARNESS_REPLAY=<response file> replays a saved model response without calling
  the model.

  The self-reference message is aimed at the repair loop. I haven't added a line
  about it to the hints, because that would change the prompt being compared.

❯ What was the form of the very long answers (9k and 15k tokens) - have these

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● Both are stored and both work. Before describing them, I'm checking two
  details: whether the extra buttonLabel in the first one does anything, and
  what the long reasoning was spent on.

  Ran 3 shell commands (ctrl+o to expand)

● Both long answers are stored, and both are working, near-ideal solutions that
  scored 9/9.

  Where they are

  - 9,393 tokens (95 s):
    a-scratch/ifm-harness/runs/2026-10-21T22-49-21-386Z/sample-1/
  - 15,545 tokens (152 s):
    a-scratch/ifm-harness/runs/2026-10-21T22-51-02-848Z/sample-1/

  Each folder holds the full prompt, the reply and the reasoning
  (reasoning-1.txt), plus the assessment.

  What they look like

  Almost all the length is reasoning: about 38k and 65k characters. The PTF
  documents themselves are only about 1.9k and 2.2k characters. Both have the
  same shape as the ideal layer: extend the image generator, add the finder as a
  hidden subcomponent sharing its view, and set state.prompt from
  {finder}.noteText. Each makes one new widget and leaves the originals in
  place, and in each the fake-Pixabay trials passed after moving the widget to
  two different notes.

  Small differences from the ideal:
  - 9.4k answer: it adds a buttonLabel $compute that only changes the label from
    "Find Image" to "Generate image of closest note". That's a harmless extra.
  - 15.5k answer:
    - It also wires the finder's tldrawHost and hostBounds explicitly, using the
      correct reference to the umbrella
      ({fluid.demos.imageGenNearNote}.hostBounds, not {self}). This duplicates
      what now happens automatically.
    - It copies a view: {$component: {shapeId}} potentia from the workspace
      dump. That's redundant but harmless.

  Why the reasoning ran so long

  The longer answer has about 130 "hmm / actually / wait" turns. Nearly all go
  to two doubts the prompt itself creates:

  1. The docs contradict the hint. The fetched IL documentation still says
     $reactiveRoot "Must be the only key in its record" (line 400 of the
     prompt). The hint says to write state: {prompt: "{finder}.noteText"} beside
     it. The model spent a long time reconciling the two, including whether the
     stronger layer's state would wholesale replace the reactive root. It
     trusted the hint in the end. That sentence predates the framework change,
     and the docs are fetched from amb26/ponder, so the fix belongs there.
     Something like: "siblings of a reactive root merged from another layer may
     be literals or references, which override that member".
  2. How hostBounds reaches a nested component. The prompt says only "the bounds
     of its own shape as {self}.hostBounds". The model kept asking whether a
     hidden subcomponent would get bounds at all, which is why the longer answer
     wired them explicitly. The earlier crashes came from the same doubt, wired
     up with the wrong {self}.

  Both doubts could be settled in the prompt. One sentence each would do: one in
  the docs fixing the $reactiveRoot rule, and one in the hints saying that a
  subcomponent inherits the enclosing widget's hostBounds and tldrawHost without
  wiring. That should cut both the token count and the self-reference mistakes.
  I haven't made either change, because both alter the prompt being compared.
  Say if you want them.

● I've updated the hints, and the docs diff is below. Writing the docs exposed a
  framework bug, which I fixed. Three real runs with the new hints all work,
  and the model no longer dwells on the old $reactiveRoot rule.

  Hint changes (demo/tldraw/js/ai-protocol.js):
  - Reactive state: overriding one member of reactive state with a reference
    (state: {prompt: "{first}.text"}) is now stated to work even though the
    existing layer holds state: {$reactiveRoot: {...}}. The other members of the
    state are kept.
  - Hidden supplier (ingredient 2): it now says the supplier automatically takes
    the hostBounds and tldrawHost of the widget enclosing it, followed by
    "Never wire these yourself."
  - {self} in a $component: a new note says {self} there means the subcomponent
    itself, so the enclosing widget should be named by its context name. It
    names hostBounds: "{self}.hostBounds" as a reference to avoid.

  Bug fix: a weaker layer's literal member beat a stronger layer's $reactiveRoot
  contents (a came out as 10 instead of 100). References were already displaced
  correctly; literals now follow layer strength the same way. The fix is in
  fluid.extractReactiveLinks (Fluid.js) and fluid.expandReactiveRecord
  (FluidIL.js), with a regression test. The full suite passes (1570/1570) and
  lint is clean.

  Check with the new hints: I ran three real samples. All three work.

● Yes. Two other fixes change what fluid-view.md should say, and I've drafted a
  diff for it, not yet applied to ponder. The other fixes don't need doc
  changes.

  Change the docs:

  1. Template handling for SFC widgets. The loader now writes the view's
     template as template: {sfcLayer: layerName} instead of using a
     templateLayer member. So a layer derived from an SFC widget can replace the
     template with its own view: {$component: {template: "..."}}. Before, that
     override was silently ignored. fluid-view.md still describes the old
     templateLayer mechanism, in the loader section and in the view layers
     table.
  2. Components that share another's view. The docs show view: 
     "{other.component}.view" but don't say what follows: the sharing component
     renders nothing, nor do its subcomponents' views, while all its other
     members are still computed. The hidden-supplier pattern depends on this, so
     the docs should state it.

  No doc change needed:
  - Root-scope withdrawal. The docs already say scope "re-resolves when a
    matching component appears or disappears". Destroyed fluid.resolveRoot
    components didn't do that before; now they do.
  - Copying the path segments in fetchContextReference. Internal only.
  - AI protocol changes. diagnose, the truncation repair, max_tokens, reported
    cached tokens and the prompt order are all in the tldraw demo, which the
    framework docs don't cover.
  - shapeId: null in fluid.tldrawViewComponent. Also demo-only.

  Your call: nested fluid.topLevelWidgets now take hostBounds from the nearest
  enclosing widget. fluid.topLevelWidget is a framework layer in FluidView.js,
  but the docs only mention it in passing and never describe hostBounds or
  preferredSize. The hints currently cover it for the AI. I can add a short
  section on it if you want the docs to carry it too.

  The diff is saved at /tmp/claude-1000/-mnt-outer-source-gits-fluid-infusion-6/
  50e9dba6-8185-4206-828d-d65fda1ded19/scratchpad/fluid-view.diff:

  --- content/docs/fluid-view.md
  +++ content/docs/fluid-view.md
  @@ -79,8 +79,10 @@
   When the SFC is loaded, its script is patched before execution:

   * `$layers` defaults to `["fluid.component", "fluid.sfcLayer"]`.
  -* A `view` subcomponent is added, with layer 
  `fluid.sfcTemplateViewComponent`, whose template is the SFC's
  -  `<template>` block (see [Views](#views)).
  +* A `view` subcomponent is added, with layer 
  `fluid.sfcTemplateViewComponent`, whose `template` refers to the SFC's
  +  `<template>` block as `{sfcLayer: "my.counter"}` (see [Views](#views)). 
  Since this is an ordinary member of the view,
  +  a layer derived from the SFC's layer may replace the template by supplying 
  its own, e.g.
  +  `view: {$component: {template: "<div>...</div>"}}`, whilst keeping the rest
  of the SFC's definition.
   * Each `<style>` and further `<script>` block is injected into the document.
  `<script src="..." data-fl-scope="global">`
     loads a classic global script. `src` paths may use `%module` prefixes.

  @@ -279,13 +281,18 @@
   });

  +A component sharing another's view renders nothing of its own: its template
  is not rendered, so the views of any of
  +its subcomponents which would have rendered into that template have no
  container and render nothing either. All of
  +its other members continue to be computed as usual. This is a way of mixing
  in the behaviour of a component which has
  +a view, without its display.
  +
  View layers


   | Layer | Template source |
   |---|---|
   | fluid.viewComponent | Base layer. Supplies vTree, a virtual DOM tree,
  directly or computed from templateTree |
  -| fluid.templateViewComponent | template, an HTML string |
  -| fluid.sfcTemplateViewComponent | The <template> block of the SFC named by
  templateLayer (added by the loader) |
  +| fluid.templateViewComponent | template - an HTML string, or {sfcLayer: 
  layerName} for the <template> block of that SFC |
  +| fluid.sfcTemplateViewComponent | Tag layer which the loader adds to the
  view of an SFC with a <template> block, when it sets the view's template as
  above |
   | fluid.selfTemplateViewComponent | The markup its parent placed inside its
  @id element |
   | fluid.partialViewComponent | Composites partial templates into a parent
  template at relativeContainer, e.g. "after:.footer" |

  the same for every request, which could be cached if the proxy caches at all.

     easoning-1.txt (+200 -0)
        1 +The user wants to combine the two selected widgets:           
        2 +1. `shape:finder` - closestNoteFinder: finds the closest stick
          +y note to the widget, shows its text, and computes distance.  
        3 +2. `shape:imagegen` - imageGenerator: takes a prompt, searches
          + Pixabay, shows an image.                                     
        4 +                                                              
        5 +They want: move the combined widget close to a sticky note, pr
          +ess a button to generate an image (based on the closest note's
          + text).                                                       
        6 +                                                              
        7 +This is the classic "dominant + hidden supplier" composition p
          +attern from the strategy hints:                               
        8 +                                                              
        9 +1. A fresh layer extending the dominant widget's layer (`fluid
          +.demos.imageGenerator`) followed by `fluid.topLevelWidget` — s
          +o the fresh widget IS the image generator.                    
       10 +2. A subcomponent constructed from the supplier's layer (`flui
          +d.demos.closestNoteFinder`) whose `view` is a reference to the
          + fresh widget's own view — so the finder renders nothing but i
          +ts computation continues, using the hostBounds of the enclosin
          +g widget.                                                     
       11 +3. Wiring from the supplier's output (`noteText`) to the domin
          +ant widget's input (`state.prompt`) as a reference overriding 
          +that member.                                                  
       12 +                                                              
       13 +The fresh layer would be named something like `fluid.demos.ai.
          +closestNoteImageGenerator` (the docs even use this exact examp
          +le!).                                                         
       14 +                                                              
       15 +```js                                                         
       16 +fluid.def("fluid.demos.ai.closestNoteImageGenerator", {       
       17 +    $layers: ["fluid.demos.imageGenerator", "fluid.topLevelWid
          +get"],                                                        
       18 +    noteFinder: {                                             
       19 +        $component: {                                         
       20 +            $layers: "fluid.demos.closestNoteFinder",         
       21 +            view: "{closestNoteImageGenerator}.view"          
       22 +        }                                                     
       23 +    },                                                        
       24 +    state: {                                                  
       25 +        prompt: "{noteFinder}.noteText"                       
       26 +    }                                                         
       27 +});                                                           
       28 +```                                                           
       29 +                                                              
       30 +Wait — the docs say for wiring member of reactive state: "A re
          +ference may equally override a single member of a widget's rea
          +ctive state, e.g. `state: {prompt: "{first}.text"}`, even thou
          +gh the existing layer holds `state: {$reactiveRoot: {...}}`: t
          +he reference merges alongside that record, the member then tak
          +es the referenced value whenever that changes, and may still b
          +e written as before, whilst the other members of the state are
          + retained."                                                   
       31 +                                                              
       32 +Hmm, but there's a subtlety: `state.prompt` becomes a referenc
          +e tracked from `noteFinder.noteText`. If the user types in the
          + prompt field... the write-back would write to state.prompt wh
          +ich is linked from the finder — writing would still work ("may
          + still be written as before") until the referenced value next 
          +changes. That's fine; this is the documented pattern.         
       33 +                                                              
       34 +Also, the combined widget should show something useful: it sho
          +ws the image generator UI. Ideally we'd also show which note i
          +s closest... but the strategy says the hidden supplier renders
          + nothing. However, maybe showing the note text somewhere would
          + be nice. The example in the docs (`fluid.demos.ai.closestNote
          +ImageGenerator`) is exactly:

● The proxy didn't report cached tokens, so I'm also working out how much of the
  request sits in the shared prefix.

  Ran 1 shell command (ctrl+o to expand)

● The sample worked and scored 9/9 in one exchange: 28,026 input tokens, 4,534
  output tokens, 49 seconds.

  The proxy didn't report a cached-token count for this request, so as far as it
  tells us, all 28,026 input tokens were sent uncached. That has been true of
  every request so far, so there's no evidence yet that it caches at all.

  If it does cache the shared prefix, about 77% of the request is cacheable.
  That's everything before # The workspace: the introduction, the four docs, the
  response rules and the hints. This estimate splits the reported 28,026 tokens
  by each part's share of the characters:

  ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┬─────────┐
  │                            Part                            │ Tokens  │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ Shared prefix (could be cached)                            │ ~21,500 │
  ├────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┼─────────┤
  │ Workspace, selection and direction (sent fresh every time) │ ~6,500  │
  └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┴─────────┘

  So it's about 6.5k uncached tokens per request if caching works, and all 28k
  if it doesn't. Only the proxy's operator can say which; until it reports
  cached_tokens, the panel shows this same estimate.

  The run is in a-scratch/ifm-harness/runs/2026-10-21T23-18-36-678Z/sample-1.
